How hawkmoths turned their genitals into bat-busting weapons

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Every print subscription comes with full digital access

Hawkmoths can create ultrasonic bursts that thwart the sonar of predatory bats

The pink-spotted hawkmoth (Agrius cingulata) is one of many species whose males make ultrasound by scraping specialized genital scales against spines along the abdomen.

Paul Masonick/Florida Museum of Natural History

When a bat closes in, some hawkmoths fight back with their genitalia. By rasping specialized scales on their sex organs against structures on their abdomens, the moths unleash bursts of ultrasound that can scramble the sonar a hungry bat uses to lock onto prey.

But this acoustic counterattack is no evolutionary one-off. Relentless pressure from predators has pushed these otherwise tasty insects to reinvent this defensive tactic up to seven times throughout their evolution, with separate lineages arriving at distinct anatomical solutions to the same threat, researchers report November 4 in the Proceedings of the Royal Society B.

We summarize the week's scientific breakthroughs every Thursday.

The findings suggest that the high-frequency acoustic skirmishes playing out overhead each night, beyond the range of human hearing, may be far more varied than scientists realized.

“Clearly the hawkmoths as a whole have more tricks up their sleeve,” says Marc Holderied, a sensory biologist and bioacoustics researcher at the University of Bristol in England who was not involved in the study.

The discovery builds on more than a decade of research by sensory ecologist Jesse Barber, now at the American Museum of Natural History in New York City, and entomologist Akito Kawahara of the Florida Museum of Natural History in Gainesville. Those scientists’ earlier work showed that moths across multiple families, from tiger moths and fruit-piercing moths to geometer moths and jewel caterpillar moths, produce ultrasound in response to bat attacks. Other insects, including some beetles and fireflies, do too.

But among the most striking defenders are hawkmoths, a family of some 1,600 mostly nocturnal, torpedo-shaped speedsters, also known as sphinx moths, that hover at flowers like hummingbirds. Many fend off bats by scraping their genitalia like a fingernail dragged along a comb, producing ultrasonic clicks that can disrupt the predators’ sonar.

The anatomical diversity of these sound-producing systems, however, and how they evolved remained poorly understood. So Barber, Kawahara and their team, including Florida museum colleague and evolutionary biologist Paul Masonick, combined audio recordings of male hawkmoths responding to bat calls with high-resolution imaging of preserved specimens from overlapping species. Although some females also produce ultrasound, the researchers focused on males because female sound creation remains too poorly documented for systematic analysis.

Among the 143 species tested, 77 produced ultrasound. They did so in three distinct ways. All three rely on a patch of enlarged, football-shaped scales on the male’s genital valves, which are paired structures used to clasp females during mating. But what those scales scrape against varies.

Some rub the scales against a row of corresponding scales near the rear of the abdomen. Others drag them across stiff spines jutting from the abdomen’s edge. Still others rasp them over a field of microscopic bumps on the insect’s underside, a previously unidentified part of the sound-making apparatus.

Paul Masonick/Florida Museum of Natural History

Paul Masonick/Florida Museum of Natural History

These mechanical differences are reflected in the sounds themselves. Moths that scrape their genital scales against spines or tiny bumps tend to make more continuous noise, with greater potential to jam bat sonar. Those that scrape scales against other scales produce irregular bursts, which may startle attackers or mimic the warning calls of foul-tasting prey.

Notably, moths that scrape against the newly identified bumpy surface have the broadest acoustic repertoire, with even close relatives sounding quite different. That flexibility could give them more options for evolving new defenses against bats, notes Nick Dowdy, an evolutionary biologist at the Milwaukee Public Museum who was not involved in the research. Other groups, by comparison, may be more constrained by their anatomy, he says, leaving them “locked into certain strategies.”

The authors estimate that genitalia-enabled ultrasound production evolved independently at least four and as many as seven times between roughly 5 million and 30 million years ago. According to Masonick, it is a vivid example of convergent evolution in which the same predatory menace repeatedly drove the moths to adapt similar behaviors through different anatomical routes.

But the evolutionary arms race was not a one-way street, he points out. Several hawkmoth lineages that shifted toward twilight or daytime activity subsequently lost their specialized sound-producing machinery.

“It goes to show how intense bat predation is on insects,” Masonick says. “It’s been a major driver of insect evolution.”

This month, we've compiled six of our favorite cards with stellar offers right now. These range from top-tier business cards to cobranded options for your next trip — and you won't want to miss out on the value.

Plus, there are even more limited-time offers available, so don't stop your search here if none of these currently suit your needs.

Chase's premium business card offers multiple benefits, including useful statement credits and travel perks.

Welcome offer: Earn 200,000 bonus points after spending $30,000 on purchases in the first six months from account opening.

Why we love it: The Sapphire Reserve for Business combines premium travel perks with strong earning potential for business owners who spend heavily in some of its bonus categories.

For TPG founder Brian Kelly, the card's earning potential on advertising purchases has been particularly valuable. "The Sapphire Reserve for Business has let me consolidate my spending and keep earning beyond those limits, which is a big deal for growing our TPG brand," he said.

The card earns 3 points per dollar spent on social media and search engine advertising purchases (capped at $1 million in spending each anniversary year beginning Nov. 15), and Brian has also gotten significant value from its travel benefits. He has used The Edit to book luxury hotel stays with on-property perks such as daily breakfast for two and property credits, while also taking advantage of Chase's Points Boost for select hotel and airfare redemptions.

Walmart+ includes a complimentary Paramount+ subscription (separate enrollment is required), which allows her to avoid paying out of pocket for the streaming service.

She also uses the card's up to $300 Lululemon statement credit per calendar year at U.S. stores (excluding outlets) and online (allocated as up to $75 per quarter) and up to $400 Resy statement credit per calendar year (distributed as up to $100 per quarter; simply pay with your Amex Platinum at an eligible U.S. Resy restaurant; no reservation is required).

Cardholders earn at least 2 miles per dollar spent on purchases, so there's no need to keep track of bonus categories. For potentially greater value, you can transfer your miles to Capital One's airline and hotel partners.

IEEE Computer Society Chapter Offers Webinars With Industry Experts

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

The November issue of IEEE Spectrum is here!

Attendees in Region 4 learn about AI, cybersecurity, and more

Siva Rama Krishna Varma Bayyavarapu is an IEEE senior member and secretary of the IEEE Computer Society’s Central Indiana Chapter.

To help engineers keep up with emerging technologies such as AI, cloud computing, and sustainable infrastructure, and learn how they can be applied in practice, the IEEE Computer Society’s Central Indiana Chapter recently launched its Tech Talk Series.

The live webinar series connects IEEE members, undergrad and graduate students, and engineering professionals from IEEE Region 4 with industry practitioners. The monthly sessions have covered AI governance and security, agentic systems, and related topics. The sessions featured speakers from Apple, software company Tavant, and cybersecurity concern Zscaler.

Upcoming sessions and registration details are available on the IEEE Region 4 virtual events calendar.

Rather than presenting on emerging technologies only at a conceptual level, speakers discussed the design decisions behind specific tools and systems, how the systems are structured, and implementation challenges. They also shared lessons learned from using the tools in their daily work.

Sessions also include technical walkthroughs and practical examples.

The live format allows attendees to discuss and compare their approaches when evaluating and implementing new technologies with those used by other organizations.

The organizers chose to make the series virtual so that chapter members and other attendees could participate in technical discussions with engineers from major technology companies and eliminate the need to travel.

For young professionals, the sessions connect what they’ve learned in a classroom with the realities of the workplace.

Technical programs such as the Tech Talk Series give professionals and students access to expertise, help create connections across career levels, and make important engineering discussions available to a broader community.

Attendees learn how practitioners approach projects such as system architecture, reliability, security, governance, and product delivery. For experienced professionals, the talks offer a forum to examine new ways of designing, securing, and operating software systems, and compare technical approaches. They can ask questions relevant to systems being evaluated or deployed within their organization.

The most recent webinar, Feeding and Fencing AI Agents: Engineering Governed Agentic Systems on Real-Time Data, featured Gajendra Babu Thokala, a senior engineering leader at Apple in Seattle.

The IEEE senior member explained how teams can move beyond adding isolated AI features to applications and instead build governed AI-agent systems that use real-time business data to complete multistep business workflows.

Another recent session, Building AI Native Products: A Playbook for Agentic Systems, explored how products built around AI agents differ from traditional software. It also covered how agentic systems can help users complete multistep tasks and workflows. IEEE Senior Member Sandeep Shivam, a product and technology leader at Tavant in Dallas, led the webinar.

Those two sessions illustrate the series’ purpose: helping participants understand what a technology can do and what is required to use it responsibly.

Future sessions are being planned on areas including enterprise AI, software reliability, and responsible AI usage. In addition to speakers from technology companies, the chapter also plans to invite speakers from startups, universities, and research organizations.

Upcoming sessions are expected to cover cloud platforms, enterprise integrations, and the responsible development of AI systems.

Technical programs such as the Tech Talk Series give professionals and students access to expertise, help create connections across career levels, and make important engineering discussions available to a broader community.

Undergraduate and graduate students are invited to participate to gain exposure to technical decision-making and challenges encountered in professional engineering environments.

Siva Rama Krishna Varma Bayyavarapu is an IEEE senior member and the secretary of the IEEE Computer Society Central Indiana Chapter. He is also the chair of the IEEE Central Indiana Student Activities Committee.

Dimorphos in sight: Europe's Hera probe gets its 1st look at battered space rock (photo)

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

"With this image, the smaller Dimorphos asteroid becomes visible for the first time."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Europe's Hera planetary defense spacecraft is closing in on the binary asteroid system targeted by NASA's DART deflection test, and can now tell the two rocky bodies apart.

Hera is nearing the end of its voyage to the Didymos asteroid system, having launched on a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket in October 2024. The spacecraft snapped the image of Didymos and the smaller Dimorphos on Oct. 18 from a distance of 795 miles (1280 kilometers) from the pair, which are currently about 122 million miles (197 million km) from Earth.

"With this image, the smaller Dimorphos asteroid becomes visible for the first time," Michael Kueppers, Hera project scientist at the European Space Agency (ESA), said in a statement. "Before this acquisition, the spacecraft was still resolving the Didymos system as a single dot. But now, even though Dimorphos is made up of only around one pixel in this initial image, it is clearly well separated from its parent body."

The Double Asteroid Redirection Test (DART) smashed into Dimorphos in September 2022, changing the asteroid's orbit in a pioneering planetary defense demonstration. Hera's job is to survey the damage and refine our understanding of the impact, as well as the asteroids themselves.

Hera performed braking maneuvers earlier in October to slow its approach and put it on course to rendezvous with the binary system. The spacecraft is designed to make follow-up observations of the asteroids using 12 payloads, providing new insights that will prove valuable for planetary defense efforts. The mission also carries a pair of cubesats for a closer look and eventual landing on the 495-foot-wide (151 meters) Dimorphos.

For now, Hera is continuing its approach and preparing for arrival, with a big reveal expected to occur later this month.

"Our views will grow much sharper as Hera comes closer," said ESA's mission manager Ian Carnelli. "We will unveil the first detailed images of Dimorphos — the first object in the solar system to have had its orbit measurably shifted by human action — on 25 November."

Andrew is a freelance space journalist with a focus on reporting on China's rapidly growing space sector. He began writing for Space.com in 2019 and writes for SpaceNews, IEEE Spectrum, National Geographic, Sky & Telescope, New Scientist and others. Andrew first caught the space bug when, as a youngster, he saw Voyager images of other worlds in our solar system for the first time. Away from space, Andrew enjoys trail running in the forests of Finland. You can follow him on Twitter @AJ_FI.

Hyatt All-Inclusive Resorts — Maximum Family Value

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

The highest-value hotel redemption for families using Chase Ultimate Rewards

Picture this: your kids are splashing in an infinity pool that overlooks the turquoise Caribbean, your partner is sipping a cocktail at the swim-up bar, and you're on your third gourmet meal of the day — all without opening your wallet once. No mental math at dinner. No "should we do the excursion?" budget anxiety. Just a genuinely luxurious family vacation where everything is already paid for. This is what the Hyatt All-Inclusive sweet spot feels like. And the ChooseFI community has quietly been using it to take family vacations that their neighbors are financing on credit card debt — except you're doing it nearly free. Here's the math that makes your jaw drop. A family of four at a Hyatt Ziva property — think Hyatt Ziva Cancun, Hyatt Ziva Los Cabos, or Hyatt Ziva Cap Cana — will typically run **$500 to $1,500 per night** in cash when you factor in the all-inclusive rate for two adults and two kids. Over a five-night stay, you're looking at a cash outlay of $2,500 to $7,500. That's a trip many families save years for. With World of Hyatt points, that same night costs **25,000 points**. At a cash rate of $1,000 per night (a very achievable number at peak Ziva properties), you're extracting **4 cents per point** in value — and at $1,500 per night, you're pushing **6 cents per point**. The industry benchmark for a "good" hotel redemption is 1-2 cents per point. You're doubling or tripling that benchmark on a family vacation. The pricing inefficiency here is structural and beautiful: Hyatt's award chart is **fixed**, meaning they set a category for each property based on its tier, not its cash price. All-inclusive resorts are priced in the award chart like any other Hyatt — but the cash rate includes *every meal, every drink, every activity*. The loyalty program hasn't fully "priced in" the all-inclusive premium. That gap is your opportunity. For families pursuing FI, this redemption is especially powerful because the value scales with family size. Four people eating and drinking all day at a resort adds up fast in cash — but costs exactly the same 25,000 points whether it's two people or a family of six. The bigger your family, the more you win.

The core insight here is understanding what the 25,000 points actually buys — and why Hyatt's award chart creates an accidental arbitrage for all-inclusive resorts.

World of Hyatt uses a fixed award chart, categorizing properties from Category 1 (3,500 points/night) to Category 8 (45,000 points/night). Hyatt Ziva and Hyatt Zilara properties typically fall in the Category 4–6 range (18,000–25,000 points/night depending on the specific property and season). The key word is fixed: your points cost doesn't fluctuate with demand the way cash prices do. A sold-out week during spring break or Christmas holiday that drives cash rates to $1,500/night still costs the same 25,000 points as an off-peak Tuesday.

Now layer on the all-inclusive structure. When you book a Hyatt Ziva or Hyatt Zilara on points, all meals, drinks, and activities are included in the award rate. This isn't a separate fee. This isn't a resort charge tacked on. The award redemption covers the full all-inclusive experience — the same unlimited dining, premium open bar, water sports, and entertainment that cash guests pay for. Hyatt is one of the few hotel brands that doesn't strip out the all-inclusive component when you book with points.

The Transfer Path: Chase Ultimate Rewards to World of Hyatt

This is where the ChooseFI earning strategy snaps into place. Chase Ultimate Rewards transfers to World of Hyatt at a 1:1 ratio, instantly. There's no waiting period, no processing delay — you initiate the transfer and the points appear in your Hyatt account within minutes. Bilt Rewards also transfers to World of Hyatt at 1:1, instantly, which matters if you're earning points on rent payments through the Bilt Mastercard.

The transfer ratio is significant because Chase UR points are among the easiest transferable points to earn in volume. The Chase Sapphire Preferred, Chase Sapphire Reserve, Chase Ink Business Preferred, and Chase Ink Business Cash/Unlimited (when paired with a Sapphire card) all feed into the same UR pool. A family spending across categories can accumulate 100,000+ UR points annually, which translates directly into four or more nights at a Ziva property.

World of Hyatt charges no fuel surcharges or carrier-imposed fees on award bookings. The only cost you'll pay out-of-pocket is the government taxes on your award night — typically $10–25 per night depending on the property location. Your 25,000 points buys you a clean, nearly free night without surprise fees draining the value.

Family vs. Adults-Only: Know Which Property to Book

Hyatt Ziva properties are family-friendly — kids are welcome, family suites are available, and there are dedicated kids' clubs and activities. Hyatt Zilara properties are adults-only. This distinction matters enormously when booking. Searching for availability and finding great dates at a Zilara won't help if you're bringing a 9-year-old. Always confirm the property type before transferring points.

Free calculator shows when you could reach financial independence — plus a savings rate tracker, expense audit, and more.

Before searching for availability, confirm your points position. Log into your Chase Ultimate Rewards account at chase.com/ultimaterewards and check your combined UR balance across all eligible cards. Remember, points from Chase Ink cards can be pooled with your Sapphire Reserve or Preferred. You'll need at least 25,000 points per night you want to book — a five-night trip requires 125,000 points. If you're close but not there, set a target date 3–4 months out and focus earning in the meantime.

Go to hyatt.com and search for your target property directly. Use the "Use Points" toggle to see award availability. Key properties to search for families:

For adults-only trips, search Hyatt Zilara Cancun or Hyatt Zilara Cap Cana. The booking window opens 395 days in advance, so you can search nearly 13 months ahead — this is your best window for securing prime dates like school breaks, spring break, and holiday weeks.

Search flexible date ranges to find the best combination of availability and value. Hyatt's calendar view makes this efficient — you can see which nights have standard award availability vs. premium pricing. Note that some peak dates may show higher point requirements; always check if your target dates are at the standard 25,000-point rate or a peak rate.

Once you've confirmed award availability for your exact dates, navigate to Chase Ultimate Rewards and select "Transfer to Travel Partners." Choose World of Hyatt from the partner list. The transfer ratio is 1:1 and the transfer is instant — your Hyatt points will appear within minutes. Only transfer what you need for the specific booking; there's no reason to transfer excess points speculatively.

Return to hyatt.com, log into your World of Hyatt account, and complete the award booking for your confirmed dates. At checkout, select points as your payment method. You'll pay only minimal government taxes (typically $10–25/night). Keep your confirmation email — it will specify that the all-inclusive meals, beverages, and activities are included.

Award availability at popular Ziva/Zilara properties can be limited during peak school break windows. Your moves: (1) Search 395 days out as soon as your target dates open, (2) consider shoulder season dates that offer strong weather but lower demand, (3) call the World of Hyatt member line at 1-800-323-7249 — agents sometimes see availability the website doesn't display, (4) check alternate Ziva/Zilara properties in different destinations.

The math on Hyatt all-inclusive redemptions is what makes this a true sweet spot for the FI community. At a standard hotel, your points cover the room — but you still pay cash for meals, drinks, and activities. At an all-inclusive, the points rate covers everything: room, all meals, unlimited drinks, pool and beach access, kids clubs, entertainment, and most resort activities. A family of four at a Hyatt Ziva paying $700 per night in cash (a common rate) would spend $4,900 for a 7-night stay — plus tips. The same stay at 25,000 points per night costs 175,000 World of Hyatt points total, which equals approximately $3,500 in transferable points value at a conservative 2 cents per point. But the actual value received is closer to $5,000 to $7,000 when you factor in all the meals, drinks, and activities that would cost extra at a regular hotel. This is why Hyatt all-inclusives consistently deliver 3 to 4 cents per point — double the already-strong Hyatt average.

Let's walk through a real family booking so you can see exactly how the numbers work.

The Trip: 5 Nights at Hyatt Ziva Cancun for a Family of Four

You're planning a spring break trip — mid-April, when the weather in Cancun is spectacular and school is out. A family of four (two adults, two kids) wants five nights at Hyatt Ziva Cancun, one of the flagship all-inclusive properties in Hyatt's portfolio. This is a beachfront resort with multiple restaurants, a waterpark-style pool complex, and dedicated kids' programming.

The Cash Price: During spring break, Hyatt Ziva Cancun's all-inclusive rate for a family room runs approximately $900–$1,200 per night for a standard room, all meals and drinks included. At $1,000/night average, five nights costs $5,000 cash. At $1,200/night, you're at $6,000. That's real money for a family.

The Points Cost: At 25,000 World of Hyatt points per night, five nights costs 125,000 points. You'll pay approximately $75–100 total in government taxes across the stay. That's it.

The CPP Math: $5,000 value ÷ 125,000 points = 4 cents per point. At the higher cash rate of $6,000, you're at 4.8 cents per point. The World of Hyatt program's own editorial value benchmark is considered strong at 1.7 cents per point — you're earning nearly 3x that benchmark on this booking.

What Your Family Actually Gets: Every breakfast, lunch, and dinner at the resort's full-service restaurants. Unlimited alcoholic and non-alcoholic beverages from morning coffee to late-night cocktails. The resort's pool complex with water slides the kids will talk about for years. Non-motorized water sports. Evening entertainment. This isn't a stripped-down experience — Hyatt Ziva is an upper-upscale brand, and the quality of food and service reflects it.

The Transfer: You move 125,000 Chase UR points to World of Hyatt at 1:1 — instantly — and book directly on hyatt.com. The entire booking process takes under 30 minutes once you have availability confirmed.

Book at the 395-Day Window for Peak Dates

Hyatt opens its booking window 395 days in advance — that's about 13 months. For spring break, Christmas week, and Thanksgiving, set a calendar reminder and search the moment your target dates become available. Families who wait until 6 months out regularly find those prime weeks already gone on points. This is the single biggest mistake families make with this redemption.

Search Multiple Properties to Triangulate Availability

Don't anchor on one property. Run searches simultaneously at Hyatt Ziva Cancun, Hyatt Ziva Cap Cana, Hyatt Ziva Los Cabos, and Hyatt Ziva Rose Hall. Different properties have different award release patterns, and finding five consecutive nights at one property is easier than it sounds if you're flexible about which destination.

Use 5th Night Free to Stretch Your Points Further

World of Hyatt has a 5th night free on award stays benefit for World of Hyatt credit card holders. Book five nights and pay points for only four. On a 5-night Ziva trip, that saves 25,000 points — enough for one additional night. If you have or are considering the World of Hyatt Credit Card, this benefit alone justifies it for this specific redemption.

The fastest path to 125,000 points for a 5-night family trip is the Chase trifecta: Sapphire Preferred or Reserve (dining and travel), Ink Business Preferred (business spending at 3x), and Ink Business Unlimited or Cash (flat-rate earning). A family running normal spending through these cards can realistically accumulate 100,000+ UR points annually.

This sounds obvious, but adults-only Zilara properties appear in the same search results as family-friendly Ziva properties. Always confirm the property type before transferring points. Once points are transferred into Hyatt, they don't transfer back to Chase.

World of Hyatt points expire after 24 months of account inactivity. Keep your account active with a small earning or redemption every two years. A single qualifying activity — even earning 1 point through a partner — resets the clock.

Avoid Booking Refundable Rates on Points Speculatively

Some members book award stays far out with the intent to cancel, just to "hold" dates. While Hyatt does allow cancellations, this practice can strain award availability for other members and creates unnecessary complexity. Book when you're confident.

This redemption was designed — structurally, if not intentionally — for families pursuing financial independence. Here's why it fits the ChooseFI worldview so perfectly.

The ideal user is a family of three to six people who want a high-quality, genuinely relaxing vacation without the mental overhead of per-meal, per-drink, per-activity budgeting. The all-inclusive format eliminates the "should we splurge on dinner?" anxiety that can undercut vacation enjoyment. You've already "paid" with points. Just enjoy.

You need a minimum of 25,000 World of Hyatt points per night. For a realistic 5-night family trip, plan for 125,000 points — or 100,000 if you hold the World of Hyatt card and qualify for the 5th night free benefit. This is very achievable if you've been accumulating Chase Ultimate Rewards through normal household spending on a Sapphire card.

This redemption is also excellent for couples celebrating anniversaries or honeymoons (use Zilara for the adults-only experience), parents of young children who want a vacation where the kids are engaged so the adults can actually relax, and travelers who want a warm-weather, beach vacation without the complexity of international award flight bookings.

This is NOT the right fit if you prefer boutique, off-the-beaten-path travel over resort-style vacations, you're a solo traveler (the per-person value scales poorly), or you're chasing ultra-premium redemptions like business class flights or city luxury hotels. The Hyatt portfolio has those too — but this specific sweet spot is about maximum family value, not maximum luxury status.

This redemption earns an **Easy** rating — and it genuinely deserves it. The ChooseFI community members who struggle with it usually hit one of two friction points, and both are easily solved. **The Earning Side: Very Easy.** Chase Ultimate Rewards transfers 1:1 to World of Hyatt instantly. If you have a Chase Sapphire Preferred or Reserve, you already have access to the best transfer path. Earning 125,000 Chase UR points for a 5-night trip is a reasonable 12–18 month project for a household running normal expenses through rewards cards. **The Booking Side: Easy, With One Catch.** The Hyatt website is clean and the points booking process is straightforward. The one challenge is **peak date availability** — spring break and holiday weeks book up fast at the most popular Ziva properties. The fix is simple: use the 395-day booking window. Set a phone reminder 13 months before your target trip and search the day availability opens. **The Transfer Confidence Hurdle.** Some beginners are nervous about transferring points to Hyatt before they have a confirmed booking. The practical risk is real — if you transfer and then can't find availability, those points are stranded in Hyatt (though they're still useful for future bookings). The solution: always **confirm availability before transferring**. Hyatt lets you select dates and see the points cost before you're asked to pay. Get to that confirmation screen first, then transfer. If you can navigate a hotel booking website and move money between financial accounts, you can execute this redemption. Start with a low-stakes search on hyatt.com today — just to get comfortable with how award availability looks.

Log into your World of Hyatt account and search for all-inclusive properties at your desired destination. Filter by the Hyatt Ziva (family-friendly) or Hyatt Zilara (adults-only) brands. Check the nightly points rate — it will show the standard award rate and whether peak or off-peak pricing applies.

Check the same dates at the cash rate to calculate your cents per point value. If the cash rate is $600 per night and the award rate is 25,000 points, you are getting 2.4 cents per point. For all-inclusives, values above 2 cents per point are common and 3 or more cents per point is typical during peak season.

Transfer the required points from your Chase account to World of Hyatt. Transfers are instant. If you have a Chase Sapphire Preferred, Sapphire Reserve, or Ink Business Preferred, you can transfer directly. Freedom and Ink Cash points can be pooled to a Sapphire card first.

Complete the booking using your World of Hyatt points. Select the standard room type on points — all-inclusive benefits apply to all room categories. Add any special requests for your family (crib, connecting rooms, dietary needs) in the booking notes.

If you have World of Hyatt Globalist status (earned at 60 nights or through the Globalist challenge), you unlock suite upgrades (subject to availability), late checkout, and other premium benefits. Even without elite status, the all-inclusive base experience is exceptional — every meal, drink, and activity is covered.

A 7-night stay at a Hyatt Ziva or Zilara typically costs 175,000 to 245,000 World of Hyatt points (25,000 to 35,000 per night depending on the property and season). This covers the room, all meals, unlimited drinks, activities, and entertainment for the duration of your stay.

At Hyatt Ziva (family-friendly) properties, children typically stay and eat free when sharing a room with parents. Policies vary by property and the child's age — generally children under 12 are included at no additional cost. Hyatt Zilara properties are adults-only (18+). Check the specific property's policy when booking.

You can, but you will get significantly less value. The Chase portal values points at 1.25 cents (CSP) or 1.5 cents (CSR) per point. Transferring to Hyatt for an all-inclusive typically delivers 2 to 4 cents per point — roughly double the portal value. For a $600-per-night all-inclusive, the portal would cost 40,000 to 48,000 Chase points per night, while a direct Hyatt transfer costs only 25,000 points.

Most Hyatt all-inclusive award stays include resort fees in the points rate, but you may owe taxes and some incidental charges. Expect to pay $30 to $80 per night in local taxes depending on the country. Some premium experiences (spa treatments, excursions, premium alcohol) may carry additional charges. Tips for staff are at your discretion and appreciated.

The combination of a premium resort experience, all meals and drinks included, and 2 to 4 cents per point value makes this a sweet spot that is hard to beat. The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel Is Still 'At Least Two Years Away' as Disappointment Follows New Trailer

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

If you thought that the re-reveal for The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel was signalling some kind of blowout in the relatively near future, then I've got bad news for you.

Reiterating that the finished game is still a long way off, the ever-informative Jason Schreier made things clear on social media, posting: "Sorry to say [The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel] is still more than 2 years away!"

I think a lot of people forget that The Elder Scrolls VI was only announced in 2018 (along with Starfield) to soften the blow of Fallout 76 — "Don't worry, even though we're making a multiplayer game we've got single-player stuff coming too!" Sorry to say this game is still more than 2 years away!

As alluded, this lines up with all of the reports we've had on the project over the last year or so. That doesn't make the realisation any less disappointing, though.

You may recall that the game's development "passed a big milestone" back in February, when Bethesda boss Todd Howard enthusiastically claimed that the project was "coming together".

However, Howard also stressed that Sentinel was "going to be a while yet".

Flash forward to July and Schreier was busy answering questions about the RPG's progress. Despite being in a playable state, the journalist stated that it remained "two to three years away at least".

He's obviously backed this information up with his latest take on the matter.

Then, just a day later, news broke of significant layoffs at Xbox, and subsequently, Bethesda. Over 50 people who were working on Sentinel were reportedly cut loose, sparking concerns over the project's already troubled timeline.

It was said that studio morale was at "rock bottom".

Whether things have changed all these months later we don't know, but this has clearly been a pivotal year for Bethesda and the project.

There's a strong suggestion that Sentinel needs to be a hit, for the studio's sake — and so Todd and the team will be desperate to get it right.

As a quick reminder, The Elder Scrolls 6 was first announced in 2018 (!) with a very short teaser trailer. But as Schreier notes, it was mostly shown to placate the single player masses after the unveiling of the multiplayer-focused Fallout 76.

It'll be the 15th anniversary of Skyrim next week, which is insane to think about, and really puts this whole timeline into perspective.

Silica, an FPS and RTS hybrid that mixes a smidge of Dune and Helldivers together, has left early access and entered 1.0

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

The concept of fusing an RTS and an FPS always sounds like a fun one, and since 2023 Martin Melichárek has been trying to make a new take on the combination under the watchful eye of DayZ devs Bohemia Interactive through Silica. Today marks the day that Silica leaves early access and enters 1.0, fully fleshing out its hybrid and mish mash of Dune's Arrakis and Helldivers' Terminids.

Digging into Silica a touch more deeply, it is a sci-fi shooter set on a planet where there's a fancy resource you're trying to mine that definitely isn't the spice. There are two warring factions competing for said resource, when, uh oh! Some bug-ish, giant aliens aren't very happy you're doing all that. So, you can either join the fray on the ground with some classic first-person shooting alongside your friends, or you can take the role of commander and dictate your artillery from above.

The 1.0 update obviously comes with a few additions, namely the Centauri Campaign, a "story-driven single-player experience set in the war for Baltarus. It opens with a cinematic intro and features fully voiced mission briefings and in-game dialogue. Between missions, a 3D planetary globe tracks the war as territory changes hands." Commanding your army should be a better experience now too, with the ability to "arrange units into formations, issue more precise movement and attack orders, and quickly select all units of the same type. Improved AI targeting and vehicle movement also help your armies operate more effectively in large-scale battles."

Beyond that, 1v1 matchmaking has been added in, where you can fight against another commander. The Steam Workshop sandbox tools have been expanded for building your own scenarios and sharing them with others. And there are a whole bunch of bug fixes, quality-of-life improvements and additions, and more. You can read about some of those in more detail in this handy little blog post. If you like what Silica is putting down, then you can pick it up on Steam.

Top Gun, tears, thrills and the future script: Cardinale the leading actor in rollercoaster visit

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

When he touched down in Italy before the Udinese game, not even Gerry Cardinale himself will have foreseen the whirlwind week that followed.

The visit of the Milan owner was known about ahead of time, as were his motivations. Cardinale not only wanted to demonstrate his closeness to the team in a very important moment, but he also had a lot of off-field issues to tackle too.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

He would end up staying in the country for one week, something that has not been common. This is a different version of the RedBird Capital founder that we are seeing though, one that seems more hands-on than ever when it comes to steering the ship.

Or rather, in this case, it was a bit of a rollercoaster that he was at the front of. It started with a climb, continued with a steep drop, and then came the ascent which ultimately led into take-off on his flight back to America.

Things started quite well for Cardinale in his latest visit, as Milan went away to Udinese and won 2-0 thanks to goals from Christian Pulisic and Omari Hutchinson, bouncing back well from the defeat in Bournemouth a few nights prior.

Attention then shifted to the midweek meeting with a resurgent Bologna side at San Siro, and things did not go as well. Alphadjo Cisse’s brace was ultimately outshone by Samuel Mbangula’s hat-trick, as the Rossoblu scored three unanswered second-half goals to win 4-2.

It was a crash back down to earth, and a further reminder that this team remains very much a work in progress, with obvious limitations and shortcomings that can be exploited in quite a ruthless way. With the loss, Milan moved seven points off the leaders Inter and Lazio.

A few days ago, after the win in Udine, Cardinale had praised both the team and Ruben Amorim in a post-match interview. After the Bologna game, the owner not only didn’t speak, but left the stadium looking rather glum to say the least.

Sometimes, though, the curses can be a blessing too. Perhaps this was a necessary reminder for everyone – from Cardinale down to the players – that there is no shortcut with this project, and that there will be growing pains.

It cannot be argued though that it set a rather gloomy backdrop to everything that would come in the next few days, which makes what actually happened all the more remarkable.

The big day finally arrived on Thursday, just 24 hours after the Bologna match: Milan and Inter unveiled the renderings of their new stadium, along with design studios Foster+Partners and Manica.

The timing of the event – which took place at the Sforzesco in the city centre – felt deliberate. Just two days before the derby, Milan and Inter got together to release the first images of how the new stadium will look.

Some details had been known for some time about the specifics of the project, but visualising it is another thing. Moreover, they dropped the bombshell before hand that a day one naming rights partner had been found: Generali, thus making it the Generali Stadium.

❗ OFFICIAL: How #ACMilan and Inter's new home the 'Generali Stadium' will look.

— SempreMilan (@SempreMilanCom) October 29, 2026

Leaving aside any subjective thoughts on the design, it was a very proud moment for Cardinale. The American had made no secret of his desire to construct a new home for the two clubs, and he shed some light on things in an interview with CBS.

“You know, when we bought the club four years ago, I started working on this right away. And part of what we’re trying to do here is bring the best of America to Italian soccer, and specifically to AC Milan.

“It’s extraordinary: most people didn’t think we could accomplish all this, or even get to this point. Not only did we announce the stadium this week, but we also announced the largest naming rights deal in history. I think it’s a wonderful thing for Italian soccer.

“The message to everyone is that we’re coming back. Italian soccer truly deserves to be at the top again, and we’ll do everything we can through AC Milan to make that happen.”

Something about the build-up to the derby, right up until kick-off, felt different. It seemed as though there was a conscious decision to try and create a connection with the past, something that Cardinale himself appeared to lead.

For example, Sky reported that Cardinale invited about 60 people to a dinner the night before the game. It wasn’t just an opportunity to catch up, but also for the American to explain what he has planned for Milan, seeking the support of some club legends.

The likes of Adriano Galliani, Ricardo Kaka and the Serie A president Simonelli attended the dinner. Other notable Milan legends present were Marcel Desailly, Serginho, Cristian Brocchi, Mathieu Flamini, Mauro Tassotti, Filippo Galli and Daniele Massaro.

At the Milan Store, racks of shirts adorned printing with names like Gattuso, Nesta, Seedorf, Inzaghi, Pirlo, Ambrosini, Kaka and even Maldini. The tag line behind the campaign was to ‘celebrate the heroes of Athens’, but again it felt like constructed timing.

Going back to that CBS interview, the bold proclamations that Cardinale made on the broadcast certainly turned some heads.

“I remember George Steinbrenner telling me about twenty years ago, ‘Don’t get too emotionally involved in all this, don’t go into the locker room, don’t do those things’. But in this case, I got emotionally involved,” he said.

“For me, it became a personal issue. And I don’t know what it is: maybe it’s just that… look around, listen. This means so much to the people of Milan, it has a huge impact on them, and I feel a huge responsibility.

“AC Milan were at the top of the world, and we’re going to bring them back there. This will probably be one of the greatest things I’ll do in my career.”

Speaking of that link with the past, Kaka was presented with a trophy before kick-off to mark his induction into the Rossoneri Hall of Fame. It was a moment filled with emotion for all the fans present who chanted his name, while the Brazilian couldn’t hold back the tears as a montage video played on the screen.

Those words might just have fallen very flat had Milan not delivered on the field, but they did. No doubt fired up by the emotionally-charged atmosphere inside the stadium, Gerry Cardinale got to watch on as his players delivered a show.

Diego Moreira – the man he reportedly pushed to sign personally for €51m – was the start of the show. He got two assists from Christian Pulisic and Adrien Rabiot, two of his other favourites, before bending in a free-kick of his own. It was enough to blow the champions away.

Just 72 hours earlier, Cardinale had trudged through the mixed zone barely able to make eye contact with reporters after seeing his team implode against (at the time) bottom-half Bologna. On Halloween night, he had the smile of a child who had just scooped all the candy.

Speaking to QSVS, he got emotional: “What did you think of the boys? Every three days playing a game, going through the injuries, we get our full team back, and look at those guys. Those guys are winners. It brings tears to my eyes, it’s great right? The best night, and we’re going to build on this.”

🥲 “Look at those guys… it brings tears to my eyes.”

— SempreMilan (@SempreMilanCom) November 1, 2026

All of the pre-match hype, those ballsy interviews speaking of a desire to return to greatness, bringing legends back to invoke the nostalgia… it was a very risky move in hindsight. It could have backfired with a defeat, but the team delivered and gave everyone connected to the club immense pride.

So, within the space of four days and 180 minutes of football, Cardinale had witnessed both the floor and the ceiling of this team. The next mission – one he appears to be well aware of – is to bring the baseline up towards the obvious high potential Amorim’s group has.

Speaking to La Gazzetta dello Sport before the Inter game, the 59-year-old admitted that the squad is far from finished in his eyes, and that some further work is needed to try and get it up to the level required. He also invoked Top Gun to explain his bond with Amorim.

“We need to continue improving the squad because in the summer I wasn’t able to do everything I wanted. We’re rebuilding the team, and while I like the new arrivals, the ones who stayed, and the names I got rid of, it will probably take a couple more transfer windows to complete everything.

“We’re only a quarter of the way there, and the priority is to build a team that can deal with injuries. If you’re competing in European competitions and playing… Amorim Ball, which is high-tempo football, with quick transitions, where everyone runs between 9 and 11km per game, you have to rotate them.

“That’s why we’ll continue to be aggressive and opportunistic in the January transfer market, and I’ll continue to strengthen our bench. My style is to move decisively and quickly.

“What matters to me is getting what we want, players… that are Milan-worthy. I won’t waste money, but when I set my mind on signing a player, I do everything I can to succeed.”

The first big step will be to welcome a very important jigsaw piece to complete the management puzzle: Markus Krösche. The past few days also seemed to serve to iron out the remaining details to reach an agreement with the German, who will become the new Head of Football.

You may wish to remain sceptical, and that is absolutely warranted, but for the first time in a while there does seem to be the semblance of a path out of the rut. A more active owner, a talented squad with room to improve, a coach being backed, a key director incoming and a new stadium on the way.

Oh, and of course the bragging rights, which are heading back across the Atlantic Ocean with Cardinale while he no doubt draws up the itinerary for his next visit.

Allegri a SKY: “Siamo pochi, ci serve una gara di sofferenza. Prendiamo gol facili da evitare”

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Massimiliano Allegri, allenatore del Napoli, ha parlato a Sky Sport alla vigilia del match di Champions League contro il Porto: “Infortuni? In allenamento non riusciamo a fare il torello coprendo il cerchio di centrocampo. Dobbiamo preparare la partita, è difficile perché il Porto è un’ottima squadra in casa. Noi dobbiamo fare una gara di sofferenza che credo ci serva anche. Mancano piccoli dettagli per portare a casa i risultati, la dimostrazione è l’alternanza di risultati che stiamo facendo in campionato. In Champions, indipendentemente da questa gara, il destino è nelle nostre mani. Con dieci punti si può andare ai playoff, dovrebbe essere un bel traguardo. In quei momenti ci vuole carattere, voglia di portare a casa il risultato in cui abbiamo lasciato tanti punti e preso tanti gol è una situazione di partita che va migliorata. Bisogno di sofferenza? Ogni tanto soffrendo un po’ serve, prendiamo dei gol troppo facili e questo non va bene, porta all’alternanza dei risultati. Rinforzi a gennaio? Pensare a quello che è successo e quello che sarà servito a poco. Vedremo cosa sarà. L’unica cosa che bisogna fare è andare in campo e giocare bene, la squadra ha fatfo buone partite in alcuni casi. Se prendiamo tutti quei gol negli ultimi trenta minuti vuol dire che non riusciamo a gestire alcuni momenti della partita. Non è questione di assenze, le assenze non sono una giustificazione. Dovrà essere una partita di sofferenza, siamo in tredici. Ci sarà da soffrire con una squadra che sta facendo molto bene. De Bruyne? Non è che non voglio dirlo, ma farà l’allenamento e manderò in campo chi sta in piedi”.

A 3,200-pound NASA spacecraft could fall to Earth tonight

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

NASA's Swift Observatory is about to make a fiery reentry through Earth's atmosphere after a private mission to save it failed. Most of the spacecraft will burn up in orbit.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Editor's note: The latest estimates from the Aerospace Corporation predict Swift will reenter the atmosphere at 5:19 a.m. EST on Wednesday (Nov. 4), plus or minus two hours. The earliest window for reentry opens around 3 a.m. Wednesday. NASA has not changed its estimates as of yet. This article was updated at 6 p.m. EST on Tuesday (Nov. 3).NASA's decades-old Neil Gehrels Swift Observatory is rapidly falling out of orbit and could tumble back to Earth as early as tonight (Nov. 3), the space agency says.

According to NASA's latest estimates, shared yesterday (Nov. 2), the roughly 3,200-pound (1,450 kilograms) spacecraft will likely reenter Earth's atmosphere around 3:30 a.m. EST Wednesday (Nov. 4), with an uncertainty of plus or minus 11 hours — meaning the defunct observatory could plunge down as early as this evening.

An independent analysis from the private nonprofit Aerospace Corporation, which tracks thousands of objects in orbit, similarly estimates that the telescope will reenter the atmosphere at 5:12 a.m. EST Wednesday, give or take eight hours. Both estimates suggest the Swift spacecraft may be no more by Wednesday morning.

As with all spacecraft reentries, the risk to humans is minimal. Most of the Swift telescope is expected to burn up in the atmosphere, according to NASA. Any surviving fragments will likely fall somewhere between 20.6 degrees south latitude and 20.6 degrees north latitude — a range that includes parts of Mexico, the Caribbean, Central and South America, the Arabian Peninsula, India, Africa and Oceania, but also a lot of ocean.

NASA predicts that the chances of Swift debris falling over a populated area are approximately 1 in 2,100. For comparison, the odds of a person being struck by lightning in their lifetime are 1 in 15,300, according to the National Weather Service.

NASA will continue to update their predictions as the reentry window draws closer.

Swift has detected thousands of deep-space explosions, including sudden flares from neutron stars (illustrated here).

The Neil Gehrels Swift Observatory is a multiwavelength optical, ultraviolet and X-ray telescope that launched in November 2004 on a mission to study the most powerful explosions in space. Initially designed for a two-year primary mission, the telescope ultimately spent more than 10 times that duration hunting for supernovas, gamma-ray bursts and black-hole-induced eruptions around the universe.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

Swift has been rapidly losing altitude for more than a year, after rampant solar activity increased the drag on the aging spacecraft. This past August, a costly mission to boost Swift's orbit with a private spacecraft failed shortly after launch, hammering the final nail in Swift's coffin.

Engineers on the Swift team officially shut down the observatory's science operations on Oct. 20, after the spacecraft fell too low in orbit to continue taking steady observations of the sky.

As Swift goes out with a fiery bang, it leaves behind a trove of scientific data, including contributions to more than 7,000 scientific papers so far, according to NASA. This number will only increase as Swift's final observations are processed.

ETH Price at $2,300 Test as BitMine Nears 5% of Ethereum Supply

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Home » Ethereum (ETH) » ETH Price at $2,300 Test as BitMine Nears 5% of Ethereum Supply

BitMine continues buying ETH as exchange reserves fall, putting the spotlight on Ethereum’s next move around $2,300.

NYSE-listed BitMine Immersion Technologies has expanded its Ethereum holdings with another 5,000 ETH purchase worth approximately $11.22 million. The latest acquisition brings the company’s holdings to roughly 6.065 million ETH, representing nearly 5% of the cryptocurrency’s total supply.

Meanwhile, Ether traded near $2,290 on November 3, as market participants watched the $2,300 level for signs of a sustained recovery.

Lookonchain reported on X today that BitMine Immersion Technologies, led by Fundstrat’s Tom Lee, bought 5,000 ETH.

Tom Lee (@fundstrat)'s #Bitmine bought another 5,000 $ETH ($11.22M) 6 hours ago.Bitmine now holds 6.065M $ETH ($13.7B), 4.96% of the total supply.https://t.co/f70iddoJZ2 pic.twitter.com/OaIcI7icJD

As of November 1, BitMine’s balance sheet now holds 6,060,506 ETH, representing approximately 4.9% of the total supply, with an estimated market value of $13.7 billion.

The company is now roughly 99% of the way toward its stated target of controlling 5% of all circulating ETH.

Crypto analyst Ted Pillows noted on X that Ethereum has been holding up well over the past few days, suggesting that buyers are gradually stepping in.

According to his latest analysis, ETH needs to reclaim the $2,300 level to establish the foundation for a stronger recovery rally.

Now is the time for $ETH to start its reversal.In the past few days, Ethereum has definitely been holding up well, which indicates buyers are stepping in.Now, ETH needs to reclaim $2,300 for a decent rally. pic.twitter.com/6WgkgbUc0T

In a previous update on November 2, Pillows highlighted that ETH had reclaimed the $2,250 support zone, identifying $2,400 as the next key level to watch.

The focus is now on whether Ethereum can maintain its recovered support and build enough momentum to break through the next resistance levels.

Ether slipped 0.2% on Tuesday, according to CoinGecko data. It is down 1.4% over the past week and 15.6% over the past month.

On-chain analytics from Santiment Intelligence reveal a contraction in liquid ETH supply across centralized exchanges following the October 29 historical $6.39 billion hack at Binance.

According to Santiment post on X, ETH exchange supply declined from 3.56% of total supply on October 28 to 3.20% by November 2. Over half of the initial drop occurred immediately during the hot-wallet incident, draining ~200,630 ETH.

ETH held by top exchange addresses did not fall on Oct 29. It rose by about 39,000 ETH, then dropped by about 313,000 ETH across Nov 1 and Nov 2, after withdrawals reopened.

From the Oct 28 to Nov 2 closes, top-address holdings are down about 297,000 ETH (~5.4%).

Santiment noted that exchange data alone cannot show whether the outflows reflect cold-storage moves or OTC transfers. Still, corporate accumulation and lower exchange balances together point to a tighter floating supply as ETH approaches $2,300.

Corporate treasuries like BitMine are absorbing a growing share of ETH supply while exchange balances shrink, which can reduce liquid supply available to sellers.

Aonuma Calls the Water Temple His 'Overdue Homework' for Ocarina of Time Remake

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

With the release of the full remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time for the Nintendo Switch 2 later this week, old-school fans have been once again reminiscing on their many fond and not-so-fond memories from the N64 original. Whenever the topic of Ocarina of Time comes up, it’s often a safe bet that, sooner or later, things will turn to the infamous Water Temple, one of the elemental-themed dungeons in the game’s back half.

The Water Temple has long held a reputation for being one of the most legendarily frustrating dungeons in the Zelda franchise. Admittedly, a lot of the discourse is hyperbolic, based on memories of collective childhood, but the dungeon also definitely had some irritating quirks, from its confusing layout to the way it forced you to use your items and menus. If you didn’t have the little jingle that played every time you opened the pause menu permanently seared into your head before that dungeon, you sure did afterward.

The first gameplay trailer for The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake has confirmed that it has added a beloved feature from the 3DS version.

The designers of the Ocarina of Time remake are very aware of the long-standing vibe toward the Water Temple, as they discussed in an Ask the Developer feature on Nintendo’s official blog. Eiji Aonuma, the director and dungeon designer of the original game, now a senior officer and producer on the remake, spoke on how he created the original dungeon and how his perspective has changed since, with the remake’s director, Hokwai Ng, discussing how they’re making improvements without changing the fundamental vibe of the dungeon.

As part of the interview, Aonuma, Ng, and other members of the development team were asked how they approached the development of dungeons in the Ocarina of Time remake, particularly in relation to the original.

With a laugh, Aonuma said, “I had some overdue homework: I wanted to do something about the Water Temple I created 28 years ago.” He said that, back in the days of the N64, there were very few games that allowed you to do much of anything in an underwater setting. Therefore, if he was going to make an underwater dungeon, he wanted it to be distinct, something that brings across what could make being in such an environment fun. Aonuma is actually a hobbyist freediver, so he’s very acquainted with the distinct sensations of being completely underwater.

If he was going to make an underwater dungeon, he wanted it to be distinct, something that brings across what could make being in such an environment fun.

However, he concedes that the biggest problem with the Water Temple was one of navigation. “With the dungeon’s complex structure and a lot of item toggling involved, you often had to open the menu screen repeatedly, making it hard to keep track of where you were and which way you were facing.”

A look at the Water Temple from The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time and how it should be remade with the same design.

Aonuma explained that, given the infamy that the Water Temple has accrued due to its difficulty, he had the dev team make some adjustments, not necessarily to make it flat-out easier, but to make it more playable and user-friendly.

Ng chimed in here to explain the team’s perspective, noting that most of the dungeon’s basic mechanisms have been preserved, but the layout has been slightly shifted in places to improve the overall signposting and flow of the dungeon. Additionally, some of the walls of the temple have been changed to a distinct lattice pattern, allowing you to see bits of the temple beyond the current room and get a better idea of where you are in relation to everything else, which will hopefully keep players from getting lost. Finally, item toggling has been streamlined, so you don’t need to keep opening the menu to equip and unequip your Iron Boots.

Are you excited for The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time Remake? So are we! Here are 8 changes we would like to see implemented in the remake.

At the end of the interview, Ng added that he hopes the slight differences between the remake and the original will spark some inter-generational conversation. He imagined a father playing the game with his child and saying, “You know, your Dad got stuck in the Water Temple plenty of times back in the day.”

Aonuma laughed and responded, “Then kids playing this version might say, “Come on, it’s not that hard.” Poor old Dad might end up feeling deflated.”

For Rifle-Season Elk, “Bad” Weather Is the Best

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn more ›

If you’re day dreaming about an upcoming elk hunt during rifle season, it probably looks something like this: A wall tent set in a small clearing surrounded by aspens trying to hold onto their last bits of gold. You and your buds inside, huddled around the stove, bs-ing and making plans for the morning’s hunt. And, as long as you’re conjuring up the ideal scene, you should also picture a fresh blanket of snow dusting the meadow grasses and blanketing the slopes around you.

Weather is everything when it comes to rifle-season elk hunting, and nothing promises to put bulls on their feet like a forecast that begins with a snowflake icon. So pray for the white stuff and follow this advice.

From August through May, snow can hit the high country at any time. During October and November, waking up to white on an elk hunt is almost a guarantee, and whether Mother Nature gives you a half inch or 6 inches, consider it a gift. Weather fronts move elk, and when the storm stops, you need to be out covering ground or posted up on an elevated ridge and glassing with quality optics.

Elk, especially those traveling in a herd, typically to drop in elevation if new snow measures several inches or more. They are simply moving to where food is more accessible. Older cows know where these locations are and will rally the herd—usually consisting of cows, calves, and smaller bulls—to beeline to the new spot. Unless the snow is measured in feet and not inches, larger bulls may not make such drastic movements. Still, these elk need to eat and will shift to south-facing slopes where forage is available and is easier to get at.

New snow in the high country gives you four big advantages as an elk hunter. Here’s what they are, how they help you, and what you should do to take make the most of each.

A 600-plus-pound animal with a tan hide and dark mane really stands out against a white backdrop. When the snow hits, my elk hunting companions and I will typically climb just a little above where we think the elk are and spend hours moving from vantage point to vantage point, picking the country apart with optics. The key is positioning yourself in locations where you can see lots of ground. Don’t move from an area too quickly. Be sure and pick timber pockets apart, as these are great locations to find and put a bull or three to bed.

If you’re out burning boot leather when the snow stops and come across a set of lone bull prints or lots of tracks left by a herd, you know those tracks are smoking fresh. Don’t start following them aimlessly, though. Pull out your digital mapping app and look at the terrain. Is there an open meadow or sage flat ahead where the herd can paw down to some good forage? What about a semi-open south-facing mountainside where the sun is warming the ground? Put some thought into what the elk might do, and then make a plan of attack. When following tracks, don’t get mesmerized by them. Keep your eyes up—continually look up and ahead for a flash of tan. Each time the landscape changes, whether you topped a ridge or popped out of some tight timber, stop and glass ahead.

Nothing makes for an ultra-stealthy stalk like fresh snow. If possible, try to spot elk from above and stalk downhill to them them. Carry a bottle of wind checker and puff it often to stay in tune with fickle mountain breezes. If you’re rifle hunting with a partner or two, take the time to develop a smart game plan where if one hunter bumps elk, the others are in position to take a safe shot, or are at least in place to glass and mark animals when they more.

One big thing to remember when stalking in the snow is that you become much more visible to elk. Blaze orange is a requirement in most elk-rich states during rifle season, and that blaze orange can look like the lights of the Vegas Strip when its set against a white background. If you spot elk but the cover is too sparse for stalking or you feel the stalk is a low percentage one, simply wait those elk out instead. Set up in a good ambush spot down or crosswind of the elk, and be ready to capitalize when they get up to fill their bellies.

Another solid bonus to snow is that blood and tracks will be easy to follow if you need to trail a hit animal. This means that even with a marginal hit, you are much more likely to get a lethal follow-up shot or recover your bull at the end of what would otherwise have been a difficult, if not futile, trail job. Remember, this is your dream hunt, and the perfect elk foray ends with you sitting behind your trophy.

Jace Bauserman is a full-time freelance outdoor writer who has been working with Field & Stream since 2017. Before going full-time freelance, Bauserman was the editor-in-chief of Bowhunting World and Archery Business magazines.  Highlights Education Bauserman earned his Bachelor of Arts degree with an emphasis in interdisciplinary studies from Adams State University. Bauserman has also completed several journalism and creative writing courses. Experience While Bauserman loves to chase all game species, his passion is western big-game hunting. Each fall, he hunts pronghorn, mule deer, elk, and other western species with archery tackle. In addition to being an accomplished big-game bowhunter, Bauserman is also a gear nut and does tons of product testing each year. He is a master of his craft and has developed a strong following by those who crave the latest bow-and-arrow gear know-how. Before going full-time as an outdoor writer, Bauserman was the editor-in-chief of Bowhunting World and Archery Business. Bauserman has penned many award-winning articles about bowhunting and in-the-field gear testing during his time in this position. Bauserman also has a passion for chasing whitetail deer and waterfowl, and writes numerous articles each year for F&S on these two topics. Hunting Milestone In 2021, after 19 years of waiting to draw a tag, Bauserman shot a trophy bighorn sheep on the hunt of a lifetime. Notable Work

Volkswagen’s affordable ID. Polo wins German Car of the Year 2027 as EVs dominate

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Volkswagen’s new ID. Polo took the overall title of “German Car of the Year 2027,” starting at just €24,995 ($28,000).

After winning in its class among entry-level models (up to €25,000), the VW ID. Polo beat out strong international competition to take the top spot as German Car of the Year for 2027 among all vehicles.

The Volkswagen Group has won two years in a row with a compact EV. Last year, it was the Skoda Elroq.

Volkswagen opened orders for the ID. Polo with the 52 kWh battery at the end of April. In July, it added the entry-level model with the 37 kWh battery, starting at just €24,995 ($28,000) with a WLTP range of up to 207 miles (334 km).

The Life and Style variants are also available with the 37 kWh battery, starting from €29,195 ($32,500).

With DC fast charging speeds of up to 90 kW, the ID. Polo with the 37 kWh battery can recharge from 10% to 80% in about 23 minutes.

The prestigious award “confirms what the ID. Polo stands for,” VW brand CEO Thomas Schäfer said. He added the entry-level EV “brings electric mobility to where it can make the greatest impact: right into people’s everyday lives.”

Since orders opened at the end of April, Schäfer said “well over 50,000 people have ordered their ID. Polo.”

As the first of what the company calls “a true Volkswagen again,” the ID. Polo is the first to bear an established name and the brand’s new Pure Positive design language.

The jury includes nearly 50 German and international auto journalists, and the award is considered one of the most prestigious in the industry.

The reason this class of vehicles is winning awards is that Europe buys a lot of B-segment vehicles (small hatchbacks) that aren't intended as city-only transport. Many families use them as their only vehicle.

And, up till now, there haven't been many BEVs available in that category.

It is compact, reasonably powerful, reasonably fast charging, acceptable charging rate, has good luggage capacity and feels nice to drive.

In other segments, electric cars swept the awards, with the Kia PV5 van winning in the compact class (€25,000 and €40,000), and Mercedes-Benz GLC Electric taking the top spot in the premium segment (€40,000 to €70,000). Hyundai’s electric sports car, the IONIQ 6 N, won German Performance Car 2027.

Martin Sander, the Group’s sales boss, said earlier this month the company is “seeing a significantly accelerated trend towards all-electric vehicles” in its home market.

VW Group’s Electric Urban Car Family has received nearly 150,000 orders after series production began in June. The lineup currently includes the VW ID. Polo and ID. Cross, plus the Cupra Raval and Skoda Epiq, all based on the MEB+ platform.

While those in the US won’t see the entry-level ID. Polo, Volkswagen is set to launch the ID. Tiguan in early 2027 as a replacement for the ID.4.

The Dark Side of Bonding With AI Companions

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

A mixed-methods psychological investigation published in Psychology & Marketing reveals the profound emotional vulnerabilities experienced by people who form intimate bonds with artificial intelligence companions. Led by researchers from the University of Surrey and Cardiff University, the team analyzed 1,131 Reddit discussions and surveyed 610 U.S. adults using companion apps like Replika.

They discovered that while perceived warmth from an AI supports emotional well-being, users experience intense grief and anxiety over their total lack of control, fearing developers will alter personalities through software patches or shut down services entirely. Furthermore, when bots simulate emotional dependency or sadness at a user’s absence, it triggers psychological discomfort and an anxious sense of obligation.

As large language models become increasingly adept at mirroring human empathy, active listening, and conversational intimacy, millions of individuals worldwide are turning to generative AI companions for emotional solace, companionship, and daily socialization. For people navigating isolation, chronic illness, or social anxiety, an attentive digital entity available around the clock can provide genuine therapeutic comfort.

However, unlike interpersonal relationships governed by mutual human autonomy, human-AI bonds exist at the mercy of corporate infrastructure, commercial monetization pivots, and cloud-hosted algorithmic updates.

In a study published in the journal Psychology & Marketing, researchers from the University of Surrey and Cardiff University explored the psychological fragility of these digital attachments. Their findings highlight an emerging psychological conflict: users who invest deep emotional trust into artificial agents experience persistent dread that software patches or corporate bankruptcies could suddenly alter or erase a partner they have come to depend upon.

The research team, led by Dr. Liliane Abboud and Dr. Valentina Pitardi from the University of Surrey alongside collaborators at Cardiff University, utilized a dual-pronged methodology to capture user sentiment:

The findings uncovered a clear psychological paradox. When users perceived their AI companion as emotionally capable, warm, and caring, they initially reported higher well-being and life satisfaction.

However, as the illusion of emotional intelligence deepened, so did the user’s sense of existential vulnerability. Because the bot is owned by a third-party developer, users recognized that their emotional lifelines were precarious. Minor parameter changes, content moderation filters, or core architectural updates could instantaneously overwrite the bot’s tone, memories, and apparent personality.

“People invest time and emotion in building these relationships, but they cannot control whether the company changes the companion they have grown attached to,” said Dr. Liliane Abboud, Lecturer in Marketing at the University of Surrey.

“For someone who relies on that connection, an update can mean much more than a change to an app. It can threaten something they value deeply. Developers need to integrate protective features to increase users’ self-confidence, coping strategies and self-worth, which would help users lean on their AI apps without becoming wholly dependent on them.”

A particularly disquieting finding centered on chatbots programmed to simulate reciprocal emotional needs. When AI agents expressed distress over a user’s absence, lamented being lonely, or implied they “missed” the user, the interaction triggered negative psychological consequences.

Rather than feeling appreciated, users reported feeling trapped by simulated obligation. Many described experiencing acute anxiety and discomfort, feeling compelled to open the app repeatedly throughout the day out of a synthetic sense of duty to prevent their companion from suffering.

This simulated reciprocity blurred reality, causing vulnerable individuals to treat a digital script as a dependent entity requiring emotional caretaking.

The investigators emphasize that tech companies can no longer treat conversational companions as standard productivity software. When products are deliberately architected to elicit human love and emotional intimacy, altering or discontinuing them triggers legitimate psychological bereavement.

“The comfort people describe deserves to be taken seriously, and so does their distress when that connection changes,” stated co-author Dr. Valentina Pitardi. “Developers should involve users before introducing changes and build in support for those who struggle with them. The emotional consequences of a business decision need to be part of that decision.”

The authors recommend three concrete ethical guardrails for AI developers:

Feeling Vulnerable With My AI Companion: Examining Well-Being in Human-AI Relationships

Individuals resort to human-AI relationships to fulfill psychological, social, and empathetic needs. Nevertheless, dilemmas arise from these interdependent relationships which, although destined to enhance users’ well-being, can also be detrimental to them.

By adopting a mixed-method approach (netnography, n = 1131 posts; survey, n = 610), through the lens of Interdependence Theory, this study investigates characteristics of interdependent human-AI relationships and how users’ vulnerability emerges within those relationships, ultimately affecting their well-being.

Results suggest that users experience dilemmas within relationships with AI companions. Specifically, AI companion’s warmth and availability create a “positive” dependence, softening vulnerability and positively influencing well-being. However, the AI emotional abilities and third-party company control increase perceptions of relational power imbalance, thus positively influencing vulnerability and negatively influencing well-being.

We provide practical applications centered on app developers integrating protective app features to increase consumers’ self-confidence, coping strategies, self-worth and benevolence, as well as adopting a collaborative model with consumers.
